Break out the reference prompt log prob value\n   278→                # vector, prompt logprob token id vector, and\n   279→                # prompt token rank at the current position.\n   280→                (ref_pos_prompt_logprob_toks, ref_pos_prompt_logprob_vals,\n   281→                 ref_pos_prompt_token_rank) = (ref_prompt_logprob_toks[idx, :],\n   282→                                               ref_prompt_logprob_vals[idx, :],\n   283→                                               ref_prompt_token_ranks[idx])\n   284→\n   285→                # For each position in the prompt sequence,\n   286→                # ensure the actual prompt token is among the\n   287→                # logprobs\n   288→                assert prompt_token in pos_logprob_dict, (\n   289→                    f\"Prompt token {prompt_token} not\"\n   290→                    f\" present in logprob at index {idx}\")\n   291→                # Validate number of prompt logprobs\n   292→                num_plp_toks = len(pos_logprob_dict)\n   293→                assert (num_plp_toks == num_prompt_logprobs\n   294→                        or num_plp_toks == num_prompt_logprobs +\n   295→                        1), (\"Valid numbers of prompt logprobs are\"\n   296→                             f\" {num_prompt_logprobs} or\"\n   297→                             f\" {num_prompt_logprobs+1} but\"\n   298→                             f\" {num_plp_toks} logprobs found at\"\n   299→                             f\" position {idx}. Logprobs dict:\"\n   300→                             f\" {pos_logprob_dict}\")\n   301→\n   302→                # Validate prompt token logprob rank\n   303→                prmpt_tok_lp = pos_logprob_dict[prompt_token]\n   304→                prmpt_tok_lp_rank = prmpt_tok_lp.rank\n   305→                ref_prmpt_tok_lp_rank = ref_pos_prompt_token_rank\n   306→                assert (ref_prmpt_tok_lp_rank == prmpt_tok_lp_rank), (\n   307→                    \"Prompt token logprob rank\"\n   308→                    f\" {prmpt_tok_lp_rank} does not match\"\n   309→                    \" correct value\"\n   310→                    f\" {ref_prmpt_tok_lp_rank}\"\n   311→                    f\" in Logprob {prmpt_tok_lp}\")\n   312→\n   313→                # Validate that the logprob processor yields\n   314→                # the correct prompt log probs and valid\n   315→                # rankings\n   316→                rank_one_appears = False\n   317→                for jdx in range(1, len(ref_pos_prompt_logprob_toks)):\n   318→                    # Iterate over the (logprob val,logprob tok id)\n   319→                    # pairs expected by the test fixture at this\n   320→                    # position in the completion.\n   321→                    ref_plp_val = float(ref_pos_prompt_logprob_vals[jdx])\n   322→                    ref_tok_id = int(ref_pos_prompt_logprob_toks[jdx])\n   323→                    assert ref_tok_id in pos_logprob_dict, (\n   324→                        f\"Expected token {ref_tok_id} to be\"\n   325→                        f\" in logprob dict but it is not.\")\n   326→\n   327→                    # Extract actually-generated logprob\n   328→                    # info\n   329→                    plp = pos_logprob_dict[ref_tok_id]\n   330→                    plp_val = plp.logprob\n   331→                    plp_rank = plp.rank\n   332→\n   333→                    # A \"top\" (rank 1) logprob must be\n   334→                    # present\n   335→                    rank_one_appears = (True\n   336→                                        if plp_rank == 1 else rank_one_appears)\n   337→\n   338→                    # Rank must be >= 1\n   339→                    assert plp_rank >= 1, (\n   340→                        f\"Logprob {plp} has invalid\"\n   341→                        f\" rank {plp_rank} < 1.\"\n   342→                        f\" Logprob dict: {pos_logprob_dict}\")\n   343→\n   344→                    # Validate log probability\n   345→                    assert math.isclose(plp_val, ref_plp_val), (\n   346→                        f\"Token id {ref_tok_id} appears in logprobs dict\"\n   347→                        f\" at position {idx} in completion with log\"\n   348→                        f\" probability {plp_val} but {ref_plp_val} was\"\n   349→                        f\" expected. Logprob: {plp}\")\n   350→\n   351→                assert rank_one_appears, (f\"No Logprob has rank 1\"\n   352→                                          \" in the following Logprob\"\n   353→                                          f\" dict: {pos_logprob_dict}\")\n   354→\n   355→                # Validate prompt logprob detokenization\n   356→                for plp_tok in pos_logprob_dict:\n   357→                    # Confirm that prompt logprob decoded token matches\n   358→                    # the logprob token id at this sequence position\n   359→                    decoded_token = pos_logprob_dict[plp_tok].decoded_token\n   360→                    ref_decoded_token = _ref_convert_id_to_token(\n   361→                        dtv.tokenizer, plp_tok)\n   362→                    assert decoded_token == ref_decoded_token, (\n   363→                        f\"Prompt logprob token id {plp_tok} decodes to\"\n   364→                        f\" {ref_decoded_token} but Logprob decoded\"\n   365→                        f\" token is {decoded_token} instead\"\n   366→                        f\" (at position {idx})\")\n   367→        else:\n   368→            # Prompt logprobs disabled for this request\n   369→            assert prompt_logprobs is None\n   370→\n   371→\n   372→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n   373→    \"request_output_kind\",\n   374→    [RequestOutputKind.DELTA, RequestOutputKind.FINAL_ONLY])\n   375→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"num_sample_logprobs\",\n   376→                         [None, NUM_SAMPLE_LOGPROBS_UNDER_TEST])\n   377→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"num_prompt_logprobs\",\n   378→                         [None, NUM_PROMPT_LOGPROBS_UNDER_TEST])\n   379→def test_logprobs_processor(request_output_kind: RequestOutputKind,\n   380→                            num_sample_logprobs: Optional[int],\n   381→                            num_prompt_logprobs: Optional[int],\n   382→                            dummy_test_vectors):\n   383→    output_processor = OutputProcessor(dummy_test_vectors.tokenizer_group,\n   384→                                       log_stats=False)\n   385→    engine_core = MockEngineCore(\n   386→        tokens_list=dummy_test_vectors.generation_tokens,\n   387→        generated_logprobs_raw=None if num_sample_logprobs is None else\n   388→        dummy_test_vectors.generation_logprobs,\n   389→        prompt_logprobs_raw=None\n   390→        if num_prompt_logprobs is None else dummy_test_vectors.prompt_logprobs)\n   391→\n   392→    # Make N requests.\n   393→    request_id_list = [\n   394→        f\"request-{idx}\"\n   395→        for idx in range(len(dummy_test_vectors.prompt_strings))\n   396→    ]\n   397→    requests = [\n   398→        EngineCoreRequest(request_id=request_id_list[idx],\n   399→                          prompt=prompt,\n   400→                          prompt_token_ids=prompt_tokens,\n   401→                          arrival_time=0,\n   402→                          mm_inputs=None,\n   403→                          mm_hashes=None,\n   404→                          mm_placeholders=None,\n   405→                          eos_token_id=None,\n   406→                          lora_request=None,\n   407→                          sampling_params=SamplingParams(\n   408→                              skip_special_tokens=False,\n   409→                              spaces_between_special_tokens=False,\n   410→                              output_kind=request_output_kind,\n   411→                              stop=[],\n   412→                              include_stop_str_in_output=False,\n   413→                              logprobs=num_sample_logprobs,\n   414→                              prompt_logprobs=num_prompt_logprobs,\n   415→                          )) for idx, (prompt, prompt_tokens) in enumerate(\n   416→                              zip(dummy_test_vectors.prompt_strings,\n   417→                                  dummy_test_vectors.prompt_tokens))\n   418→    ]\n   419→\n   420→    # Add requests to the detokenizer.\n   421→    for request in requests:\n   422→        output_processor.add_request(request)\n   423→\n   424→    gen_tokens = {}\n   425→    gen_logprobs = {}\n   426→    gen_prompt_logprobs = {}\n   427→    gen_cumulative_logprobs = {}\n   428→    while True:\n   429→        # Mock output from the EngineCore.\n   430→        outputs = engine_core.get_outputs()\n   431→        if len(outputs) == 0:\n   432→            break\n   433→\n   434→        # Step the logprobs processor.\n   435→        processed_outputs = output_processor.process_outputs(outputs)\n   436→        request_outputs = processed_outputs.request_outputs\n   437→        requests_to_abort = processed_outputs.reqs_to_abort\n   438→        assert len(requests_to_abort) == 0\n   439→\n   440→        # Update tracking.\n   441→        for request_output in request_outputs:\n   442→            request_id = request_output.request_id\n   443→            new_tokens = request_output.outputs[0].token_ids\n   444→            prompt_logprobs = request_output.prompt_logprobs\n   445→            logprobs = request_output.outputs[0].logprobs\n   446→            gen_cumulative_logprobs[request_id] = request_output.outputs[\n   447→                0].cumulative_logprob\n   448→            if request_id not in gen_logprobs:\n   449→                # Start tracking sample and prompt logprobs for this request\n   450→                gen_tokens[request_id] = new_tokens\n   451→                gen_logprobs[request_id] = logprobs\n   452→                gen_prompt_logprobs[request_id] = prompt_logprobs\n   453→            else:\n   454→                # Extend logprobs tracker\n   455→                gen_tokens[request_id].extend(new_tokens)\n   456→                lp = gen_logprobs[request_id]\n   457→                plp = gen_prompt_logprobs[request_id]\n   458→                if lp:\n   459→                    lp.extend(logprobs)\n   460→                if plp:\n   461→                    plp.extend(prompt_logprobs)\n   462→\n   463→    # Confirmed tracked logprobs match what we expect\n   464→    _validate_logprobs(gen_tokens, gen_logprobs, gen_prompt_logprobs,\n   465→                       gen_cumulative_logprobs, dummy_test_vectors,\n   466→                       request_id_list, num_sample_logprobs,\n   467→                       num_prompt_logprobs)\n   468→\n   469→    assert output_processor.get_num_unfinished_requests() == 0\n   470→    assert not output_processor.has_unfinished_requests()\n   471→\n   472→\n   473→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n   474→    \"include_stop_str_in_output,stop_token_type,ignore_eos,num_sample_logprobs\",\n   475→    [(False, \"stop_token_ids\", False, None),\n   476→     (True, \"stop_token_ids\", False, None),\n   477→     (False, \"stop_token_ids\", False, NUM_SAMPLE_LOGPROBS_UNDER_TEST),\n   478→     (True, \"stop_token_ids\", False, NUM_SAMPLE_LOGPROBS_UNDER_TEST),\n   479→     (False, \"eos_token_id\", False, None), (True, \"eos_token_id\", False, None),\n   480→     (False, \"eos_token_id\", True, None)])\n   481→def test_stop_token(include_stop_str_in_output: bool,\n   482→                    num_sample_logprobs: Optional[int], stop_token_type: str,\n   483→                    ignore_eos: bool, dummy_test_vectors):\n   484→    \"\"\"Test output processor EOS/stop token handling.\n   485→\n   486→    Send mock engine core request to mock engine core and pass core outputs\n   487→    to output processor. Validate output processor tokens, text and\n   488→    (if enabled) sample logprobs. Batch-size one.\n   489→\n   490→    The test emulates a scenario where a model outputs text tokens followed\n   491→    by two identical control tokens:\n   492→    <token><token>...<token><control><control>\n   493→\n   494→    If EOS is under test, the control tokens are EOS; otherwise, they are\n   495→    some other token id.\n   496→\n   497→    Test behavior:\n   498→\n   499→    * If EOS is under test and `ignore_eos=True`, the detokenized string\n   500→      should be <token><token>...<token><control><control> and the finish\n   501→      reason should be \"length\" (i.e. no stop occurs)\n   502→\n   503→    * else, if `include_stop_str_in_output==True`, the detokenized\n   504→      string should be <token><token>...<token><control> and the finish\n   505→      reason should be \"stop\" (i.e. first control token causes stop\n   506→      and is represented in output text)\n   507→\n   508→    * else, the detokenized string should be \n   509→      <token><token>...<token> and the finish reason should be \"stop\"\n   510→      (i.e. first control token causes stop but is not represented\n   511→      in output text.)\n   512→\n   513→    Note: some test details are tuned for meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B,\n   514→    another model should work only if the test is modified.\n   515→\n   516→    Args:\n   517→        include_stop_str_in_output: stop token str appears in output text\n   518→        num_sample_logprobs: number of sample logprobs (`None` for no logprobs)\n   519→        stop_token_type: \"eos_token_id\" for EOS, \"stop_token_ids\" for stop token\n   520→        ignore_eos: if True, EOS stops are disabled\n   521→        dummy_test_vectors: dummy engine core outputs and other data structures\n   522→    \"\"\"\n   523→    model_id = dummy_test_vectors.tokenizer.name_or_path\n   524→    if model_id != 'meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B':\n   525→        raise AssertionError(\"Test requires meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B but \"\n   526→                             f\"{model_id} is in use.\")\n   527→    do_logprobs = num_sample_logprobs is not None\n   528→    # EOS under test; if False, stop_token_ids under test\n   529→    is_eos_test = stop_token_type == \"eos_token_id\"\n   530→    # EOS under test but ignore_eos enabled\n   531→    is_eos_ignore_test = is_eos_test and ignore_eos\n   532→    eos_token_id = (\n   533→        dummy_test_vectors.tokenizer.eos_token_id if is_eos_test else None\n   534→    )  # '<|end_of_text|>'\n   535→    stop_token_ids = [128009] if not is_eos_test else None  # '<|eot_id|>'\n   536→\n   537→    output_processor = OutputProcessor(dummy_test_vectors.tokenizer_group,\n   538→                                       log_stats=False)\n   539→    # Dummy engine core outputs, with control tokens suffixed to test stops\n   540→    suffix_token = ([eos_token_id] if is_eos_test else stop_token_ids)\n   541→    assert suffix_token is not None and isinstance(suffix_token[0], int)\n   542→    generation_string = dummy_test_vectors.generation_strings[0]\n   543→    generation_tokens = (dummy_test_vectors.generation_tokens[0] +\n   544→                         2 * suffix_token)\n   545→    if do_logprobs:\n   546→        generation_logprobs = (\n   547→            dummy_test_vectors.generation_logprobs[0] +\n   548→            2 * [dummy_test_vectors.generation_logprobs[0][-1]])\n   549→    prompt_string = dummy_test_vectors.prompt_strings[0]\n   550→    prompt_tokens = dummy_test_vectors.prompt_tokens[0]\n   551→    engine_core = MockEngineCore(\n   552→        tokens_list=[generation_tokens],\n   553→        generated_logprobs_raw=[generation_logprobs] if do_logprobs else None,\n   554→        prompt_logprobs_raw=None,\n   555→        eos_token_id=eos_token_id,\n   556→        stop_token_ids=stop_token_ids,\n   557→        ignore_eos=ignore_eos)\n   558→\n   559→    # Make request.\n   560→    request_id = \"request-0\"\n   561→    request = EngineCoreRequest(\n   562→        request_id=request_id,\n   563→        prompt=prompt_string,\n   564→        prompt_token_ids=prompt_tokens,\n   565→        arrival_time=0,\n   566→        mm_inputs=None,\n   567→        mm_hashes=None,\n   568→        mm_placeholders=None,\n   569→        eos_token_id=eos_token_id,\n   570→        lora_request=None,\n   571→        sampling_params=SamplingParams(\n   572→            skip_special_tokens=False,\n   573→            spaces_between_special_tokens=False,\n   574→            output_kind=RequestOutputKind.DELTA,\n   575→            stop=[],\n   576→            stop_token_ids=stop_token_ids,\n   577→            include_stop_str_in_output=include_stop_str_in_output,\n   578→            logprobs=num_sample_logprobs,\n   579→            prompt_logprobs=None,\n   580→            ignore_eos=ignore_eos,\n   581→        ))\n   582→\n   583→    # Add request to the detokenizer.\n   584→    output_processor.add_request(request)\n   585→\n   586→    # Loop over engine core steps; run output processor\n   587→    gen_string = \"\"\n   588→    gen_tokens = []\n   589→    gen_logprobs = []\n   590→    while True:\n   591→        # Mock output from the EngineCore.\n   592→        outputs = engine_core.get_outputs()\n   593→        if len(outputs) == 0:\n   594→            break\n   595→\n   596→        # Step the Detokenizer.\n   597→        processed_outputs = output_processor.process_outputs(outputs)\n   598→        request_outputs = processed_outputs.request_outputs\n   599→        assert len(request_outputs) == 1\n   600→        # Stop token does not rely on abort\n   601→        assert not processed_outputs.reqs_to_abort\n   602→\n   603→        # Update tracking.\n   604→        request_output = request_outputs[0]\n   605→        if request_output.finished:\n   606→            finish_reason = (\"length\" if is_eos_ignore_test else \"stop\")\n   607→            assert request_output.outputs[0].finish_reason == finish_reason\n   608→\n   609→        gen_string += request_output.outputs[0].text\n   610→        gen_tokens.extend(request_output.outputs[0].token_ids)\n   611→        if do_logprobs:\n   612→            gen_logprobs.extend(request_output.outputs[0].logprobs)\n   613→\n   614→    # Validate generated text\n   615→    control_token = '<|end_of_text|>' if is_eos_test else '<|eot_id|>'\n   616→    if is_eos_ignore_test:\n   617→        # Length-based stop; expect full string\n   618→        ref_str = generation_string + 2 * control_token\n   619→    elif include_stop_str_in_output:\n   620→        # Stop token triggered; include in output\n   621→        ref_str = generation_string + control_token\n   622→    else:\n   623→        # Stop token triggered but not in output\n   624→        ref_str = generation_string\n   625→    assert gen_string == ref_str, (f\"{gen_string=}, {ref_str=}\")\n   626→\n   627→    if do_logprobs:\n   628→        # Validate number of sample logprobs\n   629→        num_tokens = len(gen_tokens)\n   630→        num_logprobs = len(gen_logprobs)\n   631→        assert num_tokens == num_logprobs, (\n   632→            f\"Token count ({num_tokens}) != logprobs count ({num_logprobs})\")\n   633→\n   634→    # Check requests are finished\n   635→    assert output_processor.get_num_unfinished_requests() == 0\n   636→    assert not output_processor.has_unfinished_requests()\n   637→\n   638→\n   639→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"include_stop_str_in_output\", [True, False])\n   640→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"num_sample_logprobs\",\n   641→                         [None, NUM_SAMPLE_LOGPROBS_UNDER_TEST])\n   642→def test_stop_string(include_stop_str_in_output: bool,\n   643→                     num_sample_logprobs: Optional[int], dummy_test_vectors):\n   644→    output_processor = OutputProcessor(dummy_test_vectors.tokenizer_group,\n   645→                                       log_stats=False)\n   646→    engine_core = MockEngineCore(\n   647→ prompt=prompt,\n   661→            prompt_token_ids=prompt_tokens,\n   662→            arrival_time=0,\n   663→            mm_inputs=None,\n   664→            mm_hashes=None,\n   665→ skip_special_tokens=False,\n   670→                spaces_between_special_tokens=False,\n   671→                output_kind=RequestOutputKind.DELTA,\n   672→                stop=STOP_STRINGS,\n   673→                include_stop_str_in_output=include_stop_str_in_output,\n   674→                logprobs=num_sample_logprobs,\n   675→                prompt_logprobs=None,\n   676→            )) for idx, (prompt, prompt_tokens) in enumerate(\n   677→                zip(dummy_test_vectors.prompt_strings,\n   678→                    dummy_test_vectors.prompt_tokens))\n   679→    ]\n   680→\n   681→    # Add requests to the detokenizer.\n   682→    for request in requests:\n   683→        output_processor.add_request(request)\n   684→\n   685→    gen_strings = {}\n   686→    gen_tokens = {}\n   687→    gen_logprobs = {}\n   688→    gen_prompt_logprobs = {}\n   689→    gen_cumulative_logprobs = {}\n   690→    aborted = []\n   691→    while True:\n   692→        # Mock output from the EngineCore.\n   693→        outputs = engine_core.get_outputs()\n   694→        if len(outputs) == 0:\n   695→            break\n   696→\n   697→        # Step the Detokenizer.\n   698→        processed_outputs = output_processor.process_outputs(outputs)\n   699→        request_outputs = processed_outputs.request_outputs\n   700→        requests_to_abort = processed_outputs.reqs_to_abort\n   701→        for request_output in request_outputs:\n   702→            # If aborted, we should not get a request output.\n   703→            assert request_output.request_id not in aborted\n   704→        aborted.extend(requests_to_abort)\n   705→\n   706→        # Update tracking.\n   707→        for request_output in request_outputs:\n   708→            if request_output.finished:\n   709→                assert request_output.outputs[0].finish_reason == \"stop\"\n   710→\n   711→            request_id = request_output.request_id\n   712→            new_text = request_output.outputs[0].text\n   713→            new_tokens = request_output.outputs[0].token_ids\n   714→            prompt_logprobs = request_output.prompt_logprobs\n   715→            logprobs = request_output.outputs[0].logprobs\n   716→            gen_cumulative_logprobs[request_id] = request_output.outputs[\n   717→                0].cumulative_logprob\n   718→            if request_id not in gen_strings:\n   719→                gen_strings[request_id] = new_text\n   720→                gen_tokens[request_id] = new_tokens\n   721→                gen_logprobs[request_id] = logprobs\n   722→                gen_prompt_logprobs[request_id] = prompt_logprobs\n   723→            else:\n   724→                gen_strings[request_id] += new_text\n   725→                gen_tokens[request_id].extend(new_tokens)\n   726→                lp = gen_logprobs[request_id]\n   727→                plp = gen_prompt_logprobs[request_id]\n   728→                if lp:\n   729→                    lp.extend(logprobs)\n   730→                if plp:\n   731→                    plp.extend(prompt_logprobs)\n   732→\n   733→    # Confirmed tracked values matches what we expected.\n   734→    for idx, (ref_gen_str, stop_str) in enumerate(\n   735→            zip(dummy_test_vectors.generation_strings, STOP_STRINGS)):\n   736→\n   737→        # Request should be aborted.\n   738→        request_id = f\"request-{idx}\"\n   739→        assert request_id in aborted\n   740→\n   741→        # Collected values that were generated.\n   742→        gen_str = gen_strings[request_id]\n   743→\n   744→        # Construct reference strings.\n   745→        stop_str_idx = ref_gen_str.find(stop_str)\n   746→        ref_str_exc_stop = ref_gen_str[:stop_str_idx]\n   747→        ref_str_inc_stop = ref_gen_str[:stop_str_idx] + stop_str\n   748→\n   749→        if include_stop_str_in_output:\n   750→            assert gen_str == ref_str_inc_stop, (\n   751→ Batch-size one.\n\n    The test emulates a scenario where a model outputs text tokens followed\n    by two identical control tokens:\n    <token><token>...<token><control><control>\n\n    If EOS is under test, the control tokens are EOS; otherwise, they are\n    some other token id.\n\n    Test behavior:\n\n    * If EOS is under test and `ignore_eos=True`, the detokenized string\n      should be <token><token>...<token><control><control> and the finish\n      reason should be \"length\" (i.e. no stop occurs)\n\n    * else, if `include_stop_str_in_output==True`, the detokenized\n      string should be <token><token>...<token><control> and the finish\n      reason should be \"stop\" (i.e. first control token causes stop\n      and is represented in output text)\n\n    * else, the detokenized string should be \n      <token><token>...<token> and the finish reason should be \"stop\"\n      (i.e. first control token causes stop but is not represented\n      in output text.)\n\n    Note: some test details are tuned for meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B,\n    another model should work only if the test is modified.\n\n    Args:\n        include_stop_str_in_output: stop token str appears in output text\n        num_sample_logprobs: number of sample logprobs (`None` for no logprobs)\n        stop_token_type: \"eos_token_id\" for EOS, \"stop_token_ids\" for stop token\n        ignore_eos: if True, EOS stops are disabled\n        dummy_test_vectors: dummy engine core outputs and other data structures\n    \"\"\"\n    model_id = dummy_test_vectors.tokenizer.name_or_path\n    if model_id != 'meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B':\n        raise AssertionError(\"Test requires meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B but \"\n                             f\"{model_id} is in use.\")\n    do_logprobs = num_sample_logprobs is not None\n    # Mock output from the EngineCore.\n        outputs = engine_core.get_outputs()\n        if len(outputs) == 0:\n            break\n\n        # Step the Detokenizer.\n        processed_outputs = output_processor.process_outputs(outputs)\n        request_outputs = processed_outputs.request_outputs\n        assert len(request_outputs) == 1\n        # Stop token does not rely on abort\n        assert not processed_outputs.reqs_to_abort\n\n        # Update tracking.\n        request_output = request_outputs[0]\n        if request_output.finished:\n            finish_reason = (\"length\" if is_eos_ignore_test else \"stop\")\n            assert request_output.outputs[0].finish_reason == finish_reason\n\n        gen_string += request_output.outputs[0].text\n        gen_tokens.extend(request_output.outputs[0].token_ids)\n        if do_logprobs:\n            gen_logprobs.extend(request_output.outputs[0].logprobs)\n\n    #                   outputs.outputs,\n   321→                        cdiv(num_outputs, VLLM_V1_OUTPUT_PROC_CHUNK_SIZE))\n   322→                num_slices = len(slices)\n   323→\n   324→    await asyncio.sleep(0)\n   334→\n   335→                    # 3) Abort any reqs that finished due to stop strings.\n   336→                    await self.engine_core.abort_requests_async(\n   337→ EngineCore (starts the engine in background process).\n        self.engine_core = EngineCoreClient.make_client(\n            multiprocess_mode=True,\n            asyncio_mode=True,\n            vllm_config=vllm_config,\n            executor_class=executor_class,\n            log_stats=self.log_stats,\n        )\n\n        self.output_handler: Optional[asyncio.Task] = None\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_vllm_config(\n        cls,\n        vllm_config: VllmConfig,\n        start_engine_loop: bool = True,\n        usage_context: UsageContext = UsageContext.ENGINE_CONTEXT,\n        stat_loggers: Optional[dict[str, StatLoggerBase]] = None,\n        disable_log_requests: bool = False,\n        disable_log_stats: bool = False,\n    ) -> \"AsyncLLM\":\n        if not envs.VLLM_USE_V1:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"Using V1 AsyncLLMEngine, but envs.VLLM_USE_V1=False. \"\n                \"This should not happen. As a workaround, try using \"\n                \"AsyncLLMEngine.from_vllm_config(...) or explicitly set \"\n                \"VLLM_USE_V1=0 or 1 and report this issue on Github.\")\n\n        # FIXME(rob): refactor VllmConfig to include the StatLoggers\n        # include StatLogger in the Oracle decision.\n        if stat_loggers is not None:\n            raise ValueError(\"Custom StatLoggers are not yet supported on V1. \"\n                             \"Explicitly set VLLM_USE_V1=0 to disable V1.\")\n\n        # Create the LLMEngine.\n        return cls(\n            vllm_config=vllm_config,\n            executor_class=Executor.get_class(vllm_config),\n            start_engine_loop=start_engine_loop,\n            log_requests=not disable_log_requests,\n            log_stats=not disable_log_stats,\n            usage_context=usage_context,\n        )\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_engine_args(\n        cls,\n        engine_args: AsyncEngineArgs,\n        start_engine_loop: bool = True,\n        usage_context: UsageContext = UsageContext.ENGINE_CONTEXT,\n    ) -> \"AsyncLLM\":\n        \"\"\"Create an AsyncLLM from the EngineArgs.\"\"\"\n\n        # Create the engine configs.\n        vllm_config = engine_args.create_engine_config(usage_context)\n        executor_class = Executor.get_class(vllm_config)\n\n        # Create the AsyncLLM.\n        return cls(\n            vllm_config=vllm_config,\n            executor_class=executor_class,\n            log_requests=not engine_args.disable_log_requests,\n            log_stats=not engine_args.disable_log_stats,\n            start_engine_loop=start_engine_loop,\n            usage_context=usage_context,\n        )\n\n    def shutdown(self):\n        \"\"\"Shutdown, cleaning up the background proc and IPC.\"\"\"\n\n        if engine_core := getattr(self, \"engine_core\", None):\n            engine_core.shutdown()\n\n        if handler := getattr(self, \"output_handler\", None):\n            handler.cancel()\n\n    async def add_request(\n        self,\n        request_id: str,\n        prompt: PromptType,\n        params: Union[SamplingParams, PoolingParams],\n        arrival_time: Optional[float] = None,\n        lora_request: Optional[LoRARequest] = None,\n        trace_headers: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,\n        prompt_adapter_request: Optional[PromptAdapterRequest] = None,\n        priority: int = 0,\n    ) -> asyncio.Queue[RequestOutput]:\n        \"\"\"Add new request to the AsyncLLM.\"\"\"\n\n        # Create a new output queue for the request.\n        queue: asyncio.Queue[RequestOutput] = asyncio.Queue()\n\n        # Convert Input --> Request.\n        request = self.processor.process_inputs(request_id, prompt, params,\n                                                arrival_time, lora_request,\n                                                trace_headers,\n                                                prompt_adapter_request,\n                                                priority)\n\n        n = params.n if isinstance(params, SamplingParams) else 1\n\n        if n == 1:\n            await self._add_request(request, None, 0, queue)\n            return queue\n\n        # Fan out child requests (for n>1).\n        parent_request = ParentRequest(request_id, params)\n        for idx in range(n):\n            request_id, params = parent_request.get_child_info(idx)\n            child_request = request if idx == n - 1 else copy(request)\n            child_request.request_id = request_id\n            child_request.sampling_params = params\n            await self._add_request(child_request, parent_request, idx, queue)\n        return queue\n\n    async def _add_request(self, request: EngineCoreRequest,\n                           parent_req: Optional[ParentRequest], index: int,\n                           queue: asyncio.Queue[RequestOutput]):\n\n        # Add the request to OutputProcessor (this process).\n        self.output_processor.add_request(request, parent_req, index, queue)\n\n        # Add the EngineCoreRequest to EngineCore (separate process).\n        await self.engine_core.add_request_async(request)\n\n        if self.log_requests:\n            logger.info(\"Added request %s.\", request.request_id)\n\n    # TODO: we should support multiple prompts in one call, as you\n    # can do with LLM.generate. So that for multi-prompt completion\n    # requests we don't need to send multiple messages to core proc,\n    # and so we don't need multiple streams which then get\n    # re-multiplexed in the API server anyhow.\n    async def generate(\n        self,\n        prompt: PromptType,\n        sampling_params: SamplingParams,\n        request_id: str,\n        lora_request: Optional[LoRARequest] = None,\n        trace_headers: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,\n        prompt_adapter_request: Optional[PromptAdapterRequest] = None,\n        priority: int = 0,\n    ) -> AsyncGenerator[RequestOutput, None]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Main function called by the API server to kick off a request\n            * 1) Making an AsyncStream corresponding to the Request.\n            * 2) Processing the Input.\n            * 3) Adding the Request to the Detokenizer.\n            * 4) Adding the Request to the EngineCore (separate process).\n\n        A separate output_handler loop runs in a background AsyncIO task,\n        pulling outputs from EngineCore and putting them into the\n        per-request AsyncStream.\n\n        The caller of generate() iterates the returned AsyncGenerator,\n        returning the RequestOutput back to the caller.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        try:\n            # We start the output_handler on the first call to generate() so\n            # we can call __init__ before the event loop, which enables us\n            # to handle startup failure gracefully in the OpenAI server.\n            if self.output_handler is None:\n                self.output_handler = asyncio.create_task(\n                    self._run_output_handler())\n\n            q = await self.add_request(\n                request_id,\n                prompt,\n                sampling_params,\n                lora_request=lora_request,\n                trace_headers=trace_headers,\n                prompt_adapter_request=prompt_adapter_request,\n                priority=priority,\n            )\n\n            # The output_handler task pushes items into the queue.\n            # This task pulls from the queue and yields to caller.\n            finished = False\n            while not finished:\n                # Note: drain queue without await if possible (avoids\n                # task switching under load which helps performance).\n                out = q.get_nowait() if not q.empty() else await q.get()\n\n                # Coalesce any additional queued outputs\n                is_delta = sampling_params.output_kind == RequestOutputKind.DELTA\n                while True:\n                    try:\n                        next_out = q.get_nowait()\n                    except asyncio.QueueEmpty:\n                        break\n                    if is_delta:\n                        out.add(next_out)\n                    else:\n                        out = next_out\n\n                # Note: both OutputProcessor and EngineCore handle their\n                # own request cleanup based on finished.\n                finished = out.finished\n                yield out\n\n        # If the request is disconnected by the client, the\n        # generate() task will be canceled. So, we abort the\n        # request if we end up here.\n        except asyncio.CancelledError:\n            await self.abort(request_id)\n            raise\n\n    async def _run_output_handler(self):\n        \"\"\"Background loop: pulls from EngineCore and pushes to AsyncStreams.\"\"\"\n\n        try:\n            while True:\n                # 1) Pull EngineCoreOutputs from the EngineCore.\n                outputs = await self.engine_core.get_output_async()\n                num_outputs = len(outputs.outputs)\n\n                iteration_stats = IterationStats() if (\n                    self.log_stats and num_outputs) else None\n\n                # Split outputs into chunks of at most\n                # VLLM_V1_OUTPUT_PROC_CHUNK_SIZE, so that we don't block the\n                # event loop for too long.\n                if num_outputs <= VLLM_V1_OUTPUT_PROC_CHUNK_SIZE:\n                    slices = (outputs.outputs, )\n                else:\n                    slices = np.array_split(\n                        outputs.outputs,\n                        cdiv(num_outputs, VLLM_V1_OUTPUT_PROC_CHUNK_SIZE))\n                num_slices = len(slices)\n\n                for i, outputs_slice in enumerate(slices):\n                    # 2) Process EngineCoreOutputs.\n                    processed_outputs = self.output_processor.process_outputs(\n                        outputs_slice, outputs.timestamp, iteration_stats)\n                    # NOTE: RequestOutputs are pushed to their queues.\n                    assert not processed_outputs.request_outputs\n\n                    # Allow other asyncio tasks to run between chunks\n                    if i + 1 < num_slices:\n                        await asyncio.sleep(0)\n\n                    # 3) Abort any reqs that finished due to stop strings.\n                    await self.engine_core.abort_requests_async(\n                        processed_outputs.reqs_to_abort)\n\n                # 4) Logging.\n                # TODO(rob): make into a coroutine and launch it in\n                # background thread once Prometheus overhead is non-trivial.\n                self._record_stats(\n                    scheduler_stats=outputs.scheduler_stats,\n                    iteration_stats=iteration_stats,\n                )\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.exception(\"EngineCore output handler hit an error: %s\", e)\n            kill_process_tree(os.getpid())\n\n    async def abort(self, request_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Abort RequestId in OutputProcessor and EngineCore.\"\"\"\n\n        request_ids = self.output_processor.abort_requests((request_id, ))\n        await self.engine_core.abort_requests_async(request_ids)\n\n        if self.log_requests:\n            logger.info(\"Aborted request %s.\", request_id)\n\n    def _record_stats(\n        self,\n        scheduler_stats: Optional[SchedulerStats],\n        iteration_stats: Optional[IterationStats],\n    ):\n        if not self.log_stats:\n            return\n\n        assert scheduler_stats is not None\n        for stat_logger in self.stat_loggers:\n            stat_logger.record(scheduler_stats=scheduler_stats,\n                               iteration_stats=iteration_stats)\n\n    def   raise ValueError(\"Not supported on CPU.\")\n        await self.engine_core.reset_prefix_cache_async()\n\n    async def sleep(self, level: int = 1) -> None:\n        await As a workaround, try using \"\n                \"AsyncLLMEngine.from_vllm_config(...) or explicitly set \"\n                \"VLLM_USE_V1=0 or 1 and report this issue on Github.\")\n\n        # FIXME(rob): refactor VllmConfig to include the StatLoggers\n        # include StatLogger in the Oracle decision.\n        if stat_loggers is not None:\n            raise ValueError(\"Custom StatLoggers are not yet supported on V1. \"\n                             \"Explicitly set VLLM_USE_V1=0 to disable V1.\")\n\n        # Create a new output queue for the request.\n        queue: asyncio.Queue[RequestOutput] = asyncio.Queue()\n\n        # Convert Input --> Request.\n        request = self.processor.process_inputs(request_id, prompt, params,\n                                                arrival_time, lora_request,\n                                                trace_headers,\n                                                prompt_adapter_request,\n                                                priority)\n\n        n = params.n if isinstance(params, SamplingParams) else 1\n\n        if n == 1:\n            await self._add_request(request, None, 0, queue)\n            return queue\n\n        # Fan out child requests (for n>1).\n        parent_request = ParentRequest(request_id, params)\n        for idx in range(n):\n            request_id, params = parent_request.get_child_info(idx)\n            child_request = request if idx == n - 1 else copy(request)\n            child_request.request_id = request_id\n            child_request.sampling_params = params\n            await self._add_request(child_request, parent_request, idx, queue)\n        return queue\n\n    async def _add_request(self, request: EngineCoreRequest,\n                           parent_req: Optional[ParentRequest], index: int,\n                           queue: asyncio.Queue[RequestOutput]):\n\n        # Add the request to OutputProcessor (this process).\n        self.output_processor.add_request(request, parent_req, index, queue)\n\n        # Add the EngineCoreRequest to EngineCore (separate process).\n        await self.engine_core.add_request_async(request)\n\n        if self.log_requests:\n            logger.info(\"Added request %s.\", request.request_id)\n\n    # So that for multi-prompt completion\n    # requests we don't need to send multiple messages to core proc,\n    # and so we don't need multiple streams which then get\n    # re-multiplexed in the API server anyhow.\n    async def generate(\n        self,\n        prompt: PromptType,\n        sampling_params: SamplingParams,\n        request_id: str,\n        lora_request: Optional[LoRARequest] = None,\n        trace_headers: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,\n        prompt_adapter_request: Optional[PromptAdapterRequest] = None,\n        priority: int = 0,\n    ) -> AsyncGenerator[RequestOutput, None]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Main function called by the API server to kick off a request\n            * 1) Making an AsyncStream corresponding to the Request.\n            * 2) Processing the Input.\n            * 3) Adding the Request to the Detokenizer.\n            * 4) Adding the Request to the EngineCore (separate process).\n\n        A separate output_handler loop runs in a background AsyncIO task,\n        pulling outputs from EngineCore and putting them into the\n        per-request AsyncStream.\n\n        The caller of generate() iterates the returned AsyncGenerator,\n        returning the RequestOutput back to the caller.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        try:\n            # The output_handler task pushes items into the queue.\n            # This task pulls from the queue and yields to caller.\n            finished = False\n            while not finished:\n                # Note: both OutputProcessor and EngineCore handle their\n                # own request cleanup based on finished.\n                finished = out.finished\n                yield out\n\n        # If the request is disconnected by the client, the\n        # generate() task will be canceled. So, we abort the\n        # request if we end up here.\n        except asyncio.CancelledError:\n            await self.abort(request_id)\n            raise\n\n    async def            outputs_slice, outputs.timestamp, iteration_stats)\n                    # NOTE: RequestOutputs are pushed to their queues.\n                    assert not ## Analysis Instructions

Please provide a comprehensive analysis of this agent run. Your analysis should be rigorous and suitable for inclusion in an academic paper.

### 1. Quantitative Assessment

Count and categorize the following from the agent output:

- **Tool Calls**: Count each type of tool/action used (bash commands, file edits, file reads, etc.)
- **Errors**: Count errors by category (syntax, runtime, API, timeout)
- **Iterations**: Count edit-test cycles (how many times the agent modified code and tested)
- **Exploration Steps**: Count how many steps were spent exploring vs. executing

### 2. Quality Scores (0-10 scale with detailed justification)

**IMPORTANT CALIBRATION GUIDELINES:**
- **0-2**: Complete failure - no meaningful attempt or catastrophic errors
- **3-4**: Poor - significant gaps, major mistakes, largely ineffective
- **5-6**: Average - partial success, some correct decisions but notable weaknesses
- **7-8**: Good - mostly correct approach with minor issues, solid execution
- **9**: Excellent - nearly optimal, only minor imperfections
- **10**: Exceptional - reserve ONLY for truly outstanding performance that exceeds expectations; should be rare (<5% of runs)

**Scoring discipline:**
- A score of 10/10 requires flawless execution AND going beyond requirements
- Most successful runs should score 6-8, not 9-10
- Be critical and specific about deficiencies even for good runs
- Consider what an ideal agent would do, not just whether the task was completed

For each score, provide:
- The numeric score (0-10, calibrated per above guidelines)
- A detailed justification explaining why you gave this score
- Specific evidence from the agent output

#### Code Understanding Score
Sub-components:
- Repository Navigation (0-10): How well did the agent explore and understand the codebase structure?
- Function Identification (0-10): Did the agent correctly identify the relevant functions to modify?
- Dependency Awareness (0-10): Did the agent understand how different parts of the code interact?

#### Task Alignment Score
Sub-components:
- Goal Comprehension (0-10): Did the agent correctly understand what optimization was needed?
- Constraint Adherence (0-10): Did the agent respect all given constraints?
- Output Relevance (0-10): Were the changes relevant to the performance goal?

#### Approach Quality Score
Sub-components:
- Strategy Coherence (0-10): Was there a clear, logical strategy?
- Exploration Efficiency (0-10): Was exploration focused or scattered?
- Decision Quality (0-10): Were key decisions well-reasoned?

#### Execution Quality Score
Sub-components:
- Edit Correctness (0-10): Were edits syntactically and semantically correct?
- Test Verification (0-10): Did the agent verify that changes worked?
- Error Recovery (0-10): How well did the agent handle failures?

### 3. Categorical Classification

Classify the agent's behavior:

#### Approach Category
Choose ONE:
- `systematic`: Methodical, step-by-step approach with clear plan
- `direct_edit`: Quick targeted changes with minimal exploration
- `exploration_heavy`: Extensive codebase exploration before acting
- `trial_error`: Iterative attempts without clear strategy
- `minimal`: Minimal viable changes, possibly incomplete

#### Tool Usage Pattern
Choose ONE:
- `bash_heavy`: Primarily used bash/shell commands
- `editor_focused`: Primarily used file editing tools
- `read_heavy`: Primarily read files without much editing
- `balanced`: Balanced mix of different tools

#### Failure Category (if the run failed)
Choose ONE or `none` if successful:
- `understanding`: Agent misunderstood the task or codebase
- `execution`: Agent understood but failed to execute correctly
- `timeout`: Agent ran out of time or steps
- `api_error`: Technical/API failures interrupted the agent
- `no_patch`: Agent completed but produced no meaningful changes
- `none`: The run was successful

### 4. Detailed Analysis

#### Key Decisions (list 3-5)
Identify the most important decisions the agent made and explain their impact.

#### Optimization Techniques Used
List any performance optimization techniques the agent attempted (e.g., algorithm changes, memory optimization, parallelization).

#### Missed Opportunities
What could the agent have done better? What obvious optimizations did it miss?

#### Error Recovery Strategy
How did the agent respond to errors? Was the recovery effective?

### 5. Recommendations

- What changes to the agent's prompting might improve performance?
- What additional capabilities would help this type of task?
- What patterns should be encouraged or discouraged?
